# 04. OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Map OpenShift platform services to OpenShift AI services and shared responsibilities
---
## Executive definition
OpenShift Container Platform supplies the secured Kubernetes application platform; OpenShift AI is an add-on product that introduces AI-focused workflows, operators, interfaces, serving, pipelines, and model lifecycle capabilities.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Explain dependency boundaries.
- Plan shared cluster services.
- Define platform and AI team responsibilities.
- Troubleshoot by platform layer.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Platform Layering
- Define what **platform layering** means for OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for platform layering.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for platform layering.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for platform layering, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for platform layering.
- Review how platform layering changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: OpenShift API
- **Purpose:** Describe why OpenShift API is relevant to OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by OpenShift API.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by OpenShift API.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by OpenShift API.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for OpenShift API.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for OpenShift API.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for OpenShift API.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "rhoai-vs-ocp"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: rhoai-vs-ocp
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/rhoai-vs-ocp
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Map OpenShift platform services to OpenShift AI services and shared responsibilities
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for OpenShift API, with explicit consideration of platform layering.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for nodes, with explicit consideration of Kubernetes primitives.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for Operators, with explicit consideration of operator lifecycle.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for OLM, with explicit consideration of storage.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for Routes, with explicit consideration of networking.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for Services, with explicit consideration of identity.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform, using OpenShift API as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate platform layering, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate Kubernetes primitives, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate operator lifecycle, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate storage, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate networking, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate identity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate monitoring, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate AI-specific user experience, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The OpenShift API state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to Kubernetes primitives.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 2: Validate identity and group mappings
**Goal:** Validate identity and group mappings for OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform, using nodes as the primary observation point.

7. Perform step 7 for validate identity and group mappings; validate AI-specific user experience, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for validate identity and group mappings; validate platform layering, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.

- The nodes state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to operator lifecycle.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform must implement documented identity and access controls for OpenShift API.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for operator lifecycle, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform and is first observed around Routes.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass Kubernetes primitives controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform and is first observed around OpenShift AI Operator.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass operator lifecycle controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 3: Workbench Remains Pending
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform and is first observed around Operators.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Data distribution or quality shift.
- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass storage controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 4: Pvc Does Not Bind
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform and is first observed around object storage.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Namespace or project mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get datasciencecluster -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc logs <pod> -n <namespace> --all-containers`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass networking controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **First command:** `oc get dscinitialization -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc get resourcequota,limitrange -A`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Corporate scenario drills
### Drill 1: Regulated Financial Service
- **Situation:** A regulated financial service must implement OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform with emphasis on platform layering.

- Separate OpenShift platform, OpenShift AI, external service, model, data, and application responsibilities.
- Use declarative configuration, least privilege, isolation, observability, lifecycle gates, and tested recovery.
- State trade-offs, measurable acceptance criteria, evidence, ownership, and a rollback path.
- Avoid unsupported assumptions and confirm exact version, API stability, and operator dependencies.
## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform, relate AI platform to OpenShift API, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **OpenShift AI vs OpenShift Container Platform** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
